**Forge objects the spike will create**

cat > forge.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Forge client for spike 1431: every REST and GraphQL call goes through here and is
// appended to calllog.jsonl with its time, method, endpoint or operation, and rate-limit answers.
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const LOG = process.env.CALL_LOG || path.join(__dirname, 'calllog.jsonl');
const TOKEN = () => {
  const t = process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN;
  if (!t) throw new Error('GITHUB_TOKEN unset');
  return t;
};

function rl(h) {
  return {
    resource: h.get('x-ratelimit-resource'), limit: h.get('x-ratelimit-limit'),
    remaining: h.get('x-ratelimit-remaining'), used: h.get('x-ratelimit-used'),
    reset: h.get('x-ratelimit-reset'),
  };
}

function log(entry) { fs.appendFileSync(LOG, JSON.stringify(entry) + '\n'); }

// tag: { purpose, card } — purpose separates production calls (setup, mirror-read, mirror-write)
// from instrumentation (readback, measure, edit-drift).
async function rest(method, endpoint, body, tag = {}) {
  const t0 = new Date();
  const res = await fetch('https://api.github.com' + endpoint, {
    method,
    headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${TOKEN()}`, accept: 'application/vnd.github+json',
      'x-github-api-version': '2022-11-28', 'user-agent': 'spike-1431' },
    body: body ? JSON.stringify(body) : undefined,
  });
  const text = await res.text();
  log({ ts: t0.toISOString(), ms: Date.now() - t0, api: 'rest', rw: method === 'GET' ? 'read' : 'write',
    method, endpoint, status: res.status, ratelimit: rl(res.headers), ...tag });
  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`${method} ${endpoint} ${res.status}: ${text}`);
  return text ? JSON.parse(text) : null;
}

// op: the GraphQL operation name; rw: 'read' for queries, 'write' for mutations.
async function graphql(op, query, variables, tag = {}) {
  const t0 = new Date();
  const res = await fetch('https://api.github.com/graphql', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${TOKEN()}`, 'user-agent': 'spike-1431',
      'content-type': 'application/json' },
    body: JSON.stringify({ query, variables }),
  });
  const json = await res.json();
  const rw = /^\s*mutation/.test(query) ? 'write' : 'read';
  log({ ts: t0.toISOString(), ms: Date.now() - t0, api: 'graphql', rw, method: 'POST', operation: op,
    status: res.status, ratelimit: rl(res.headers), cost: json?.data?.rateLimit?.cost ?? null,
    errors: json.errors ? json.errors.map((e) => e.type || e.message) : undefined, ...tag });
  if (!res.ok || json.errors) throw new Error(`${op}: ${res.status} ${JSON.stringify(json.errors)}`);
  return json.data;
}

module.exports = { rest, graphql, log };
EOF
cat > board.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Board reads and writes for spike 1431's scratch Projects v2 board: read one item's
// fields, set Status and Escalation, and read every item in one paginated query.
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { graphql } = require('./forge');
const BOARD_FILE = process.env.BOARD_FILE || path.join(__dirname, 'board.json');
const board = () => JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(BOARD_FILE, 'utf8'));

const READ_ITEM = `query ReadItem($id: ID!) {
  node(id: $id) { ... on ProjectV2Item {
    status: fieldValueByName(name: "Status") { ... on ProjectV2ItemFieldSingleSelectValue { name } }
    escalation: fieldValueByName(name: "Escalation") { ... on ProjectV2ItemFieldTextValue { text } }
  } }
  rateLimit { cost remaining used }
}`;

async function readItem(card, tag) {
  const b = board();
  const d = await graphql('ReadItem', READ_ITEM, { id: b.items[card] }, { card, ...tag });
  return { column: d.node?.status?.name ?? null, escalation: d.node?.escalation?.text ?? '' };
}

const SET_STATUS = `mutation SetStatus($p: ID!, $i: ID!, $f: ID!, $o: String!) {
  updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: { projectId: $p, itemId: $i, fieldId: $f,
    value: { singleSelectOptionId: $o } }) { projectV2Item { id } } }`;
const SET_TEXT = `mutation SetEscalation($p: ID!, $i: ID!, $f: ID!, $t: String!) {
  updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: { projectId: $p, itemId: $i, fieldId: $f,
    value: { text: $t } }) { projectV2Item { id } } }`;
const CLEAR = `mutation ClearEscalation($p: ID!, $i: ID!, $f: ID!) {
  clearProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: { projectId: $p, itemId: $i, fieldId: $f }) { projectV2Item { id } } }`;

async function setColumn(card, column, tag) {
  const b = board();
  await graphql('SetStatus', SET_STATUS, { p: b.projectId, i: b.items[card], f: b.statusFieldId,
    o: b.statusOptions[column] }, { card, column, ...tag });
}

async function setEscalation(card, text, tag) {
  const b = board();
  if (text) await graphql('SetEscalation', SET_TEXT, { p: b.projectId, i: b.items[card], f: b.escalationFieldId, t: text }, { card, ...tag });
  else await graphql('ClearEscalation', CLEAR, { p: b.projectId, i: b.items[card], f: b.escalationFieldId }, { card, ...tag });
}

const SCAN = `query ScanBoard($p: ID!, $after: String) {
  node(id: $p) { ... on ProjectV2 { items(first: 100, after: $after) {
    pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor }
    nodes { id content { ... on Issue { number } }
      status: fieldValueByName(name: "Status") { ... on ProjectV2ItemFieldSingleSelectValue { name } }
      escalation: fieldValueByName(name: "Escalation") { ... on ProjectV2ItemFieldTextValue { text } } } } } }
  rateLimit { cost remaining used }
}`;

async function scanBoard(tag) {
  const b = board();
  const out = {};
  let after = null;
  do {
    const d = await graphql('ScanBoard', SCAN, { p: b.projectId, after }, tag);
    const items = d.node.items;
    for (const n of items.nodes) out[n.content.number] = { column: n.status?.name ?? null, escalation: n.escalation?.text ?? '' };
    after = items.pageInfo.hasNextPage ? items.pageInfo.endCursor : null;
  } while (after);
  return out;
}

module.exports = { readItem, setColumn, setEscalation, scanBoard, board };
EOF
echo ok

=== [9] issued 2026-11-19T15:53:58.259Z  answered 2026-11-19T15:53:58.442Z  Bash
description: Write activity, workflow, worker and helper scripts
S=~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer; cd $S && cat > activities.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: mirrorCard activity for spike 1431: reads the card's board item, writes each field that
// differs from the workflow's full state, waits, reads back, and logs the run. File gates let the
// spike place an edit or a kill at a chosen point inside one run.
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { Context } = require('@temporalio/activity');
const { readItem, setColumn, setEscalation } = require('./board');

const DIR = __dirname;
const GATES = process.env.GATE_DIR || path.join(DIR, 'gates');
const RUNS = path.join(DIR, 'mirror-runs.jsonl');
const READBACK_DELAY_MS = Number(process.env.READBACK_DELAY_MS || 3000);
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
const now = () => new Date().toISOString();
const note = (e) => fs.appendFileSync(RUNS, JSON.stringify({ ts: now(), ...e }) + '\n');

// If gates/<card>.<point> exists: rename it to .<point>.held, then wait (heartbeating) until
// gates/<card>.release exists, consume it, and go on. One-shot per arming.
async function gate(card, point) {
  const armed = path.join(GATES, `${card}.${point}`);
  if (!fs.existsSync(armed)) return;
  fs.renameSync(armed, `${armed}.held`);
  note({ card, event: 'gate-held', point, pid: process.pid });
  const release = path.join(GATES, `${card}.release`);
  while (!fs.existsSync(release)) { Context.current().heartbeat(point); await sleep(250); }
  fs.rmSync(release);
  fs.rmSync(`${armed}.held`, { force: true });
  note({ card, event: 'gate-released', point });
}

async function mirrorCard({ card, state, why }) {
  const info = Context.current().info;
  const tag = { attempt: info.attempt, why };
  note({ card, event: 'start', why, attempt: info.attempt, workflowState: state, pid: process.pid });
  const before = await readItem(card, { purpose: 'mirror-read', ...tag });
  note({ card, event: 'read', before });
  await gate(card, 'after-read');
  const writes = [];
  if (before.column !== state.column) {
    await setColumn(card, state.column, { purpose: 'mirror-write', ...tag });
    writes.push('Status');
    note({ card, event: 'wrote', field: 'Status', value: state.column });
    await gate(card, 'after-write1');
  }
  if ((before.escalation || '') !== (state.escalation || '')) {
    await setEscalation(card, state.escalation, { purpose: 'mirror-write', ...tag });
    writes.push('Escalation');
    note({ card, event: 'wrote', field: 'Escalation', value: state.escalation });
  }
  await gate(card, 'after-writes');
  await sleep(READBACK_DELAY_MS);
  const after = await readItem(card, { purpose: 'readback', ...tag });
  const match = after.column === state.column && (after.escalation || '') === (state.escalation || '');
  note({ card, event: 'end', why, attempt: info.attempt, writes, after, workflowState: state, match,
    readbackDelayMs: READBACK_DELAY_MS });
  return { before, writes, after, match };
}

module.exports = { mirrorCard };
EOF
cat > workflows.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: cardWorkflow for spike 1431: the card's truth. Holds column and escalation, advances on
// a signal, escalates on entering escalateIn and waits for an answer signal, and re-runs the
// mirror every reconcileMs while it waits, which is its drift reconcile.
const wf = require('@temporalio/workflow');

const COLUMNS = ['Ready', 'Coding', 'Review', 'Owner', 'Done'];
const { mirrorCard } = wf.proxyActivities({
  startToCloseTimeout: '2 minutes',
  heartbeatTimeout: '10 seconds',
  retry: { initialInterval: '1 second', maximumAttempts: 10 },
});
const advance = wf.defineSignal('advance');
const answer = wf.defineSignal('answer');
const stateQuery = wf.defineQuery('state');

async function cardWorkflow({ card, reconcileMs, escalateIn }) {
  let state = { column: 'Ready', escalation: '' };
  let advances = 0;
  let answered = null;
  let escalated = false;
  wf.setHandler(advance, () => { advances += 1; });
  wf.setHandler(answer, (a) => { answered = a; });
  wf.setHandler(stateQuery, () => state);
  const next = () => COLUMNS[COLUMNS.indexOf(state.column) + 1];

  await mirrorCard({ card, state, why: 'transition' });
  while (state.column !== 'Done') {
    if (state.column === escalateIn && !escalated) {
      escalated = true;
      state = { ...state, escalation: 'ambiguous: waiting on owner answer' };
      await mirrorCard({ card, state, why: 'transition' });
    }
    const waiting = state.escalation ? () => answered !== null : () => advances > 0;
    if (!(await wf.condition(waiting, reconcileMs))) {
      await mirrorCard({ card, state, why: 'reconcile' });
      continue;
    }
    if (state.escalation) answered = null; else advances -= 1;
    state = { column: next(), escalation: '' };
    await mirrorCard({ card, state, why: 'transition' });
  }
  return state;
}

module.exports = { cardWorkflow };
EOF
cat > worker.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: The spike's single Temporal worker. Reads GITHUB_TOKEN from stdin's first line when it
// is unset, so the token never lands in a file or a command line on the remote host.
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { Worker, NativeConnection } = require('@temporalio/worker');
const activities = require('./activities');

async function main() {
  if (!process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN) process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN = fs.readFileSync(0, 'utf8').split('\n')[0].trim();
  fs.appendFileSync(path.join(__dirname, 'pids.log'), `${new Date().toISOString()} worker pid=${process.pid}\n`);
  const connection = await NativeConnection.connect({ address: process.env.TEMPORAL_ADDRESS || 'localhost:7243' });
  const worker = await Worker.create({ connection, taskQueue: 'spike-1431',
    workflowsPath: require.resolve('./workflows'), activities });
  await worker.run();
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF
cat > start.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Starts one cardWorkflow per card number given, workflow id = the card.
// Usage: node start.js <reconcileMs> <escalateIn|-> <card>...
const { Client, Connection } = require('@temporalio/client');
const { board } = require('./board');
async function main() {
  const [reconcileMs, escalateIn, ...cards] = process.argv.slice(2);
  const client = new Client({ connection: await Connection.connect({ address: process.env.TEMPORAL_ADDRESS || 'localhost:7243' }) });
  for (const c of cards) {
    const id = `${board().repo}#${c}`;
    await client.workflow.start('cardWorkflow', { taskQueue: 'spike-1431', workflowId: id,
      args: [{ card: c, reconcileMs: Number(reconcileMs), escalateIn: escalateIn === '-' ? null : escalateIn }] });
    console.log(new Date().toISOString(), 'started', id);
  }
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF

cat > watch.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Polls one card's board item every intervalMs until its column equals the target, and
// prints the elapsed time from a given start instant. Usage: node watch.js <card> <column> <startIso> [intervalMs] [timeoutMs]
const { readItem } = require('./board');
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
async function main() {
  const [card, column, startIso, intervalMs = '1000', timeoutMs = '180000'] = process.argv.slice(2);
  const t0 = Date.parse(startIso);
  for (;;) {
    const at = Date.now();
    const s = await readItem(card, { purpose: 'measure' });
    if (s.column === column) {
      console.log(JSON.stringify({ card, column, seenAt: new Date(at).toISOString(), elapsedMs: at - t0, state: s }));
      return;
    }
    if (at - t0 > Number(timeoutMs)) { console.log(JSON.stringify({ card, column, timedOut: true, lastState: s, elapsedMs: at - t0 })); process.exitCode = 2; return; }
    await sleep(Number(intervalMs));
  }
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF

  .catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });

p='activities.js'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("const note = (e) => fs.appendFileSync(RUNS, JSON.stringify({ ts: now(), ...e }) + '\\n');",
"const note = (e) => {\n  fs.appendFileSync(RUNS, JSON.stringify({ ts: now(), ...e }) + '\\n');\n  try { Context.current().heartbeat(e.event); } catch { /* outside an activity */ }\n};")
s=s.replace("  await sleep(READBACK_DELAY_MS);\n","  for (let w = 0; w < READBACK_DELAY_MS; w += 1000) { Context.current().heartbeat('readback-wait'); await sleep(Math.min(1000, READBACK_DELAY_MS - w)); }\n")
open(p,'w').write(s)

grep -n heartbeat activities.js
cat > setup.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Creates spike 1431's scratch forge objects: a private user repository, a user-owned
// Projects v2 board linked to it with Status options Ready..Done and an Escalation text field,
// and N issues added to the board. Writes their ids to board.json. Usage: node setup.js <N>
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { rest, graphql } = require('./forge');
const OWNER = 'williacj';
const REPO = 'rigger-1431-mirror-scratch';
const COLUMNS = ['Ready', 'Coding', 'Review', 'Owner', 'Done'];
const COLORS = ['GRAY', 'BLUE', 'YELLOW', 'ORANGE', 'GREEN'];
const OUT = path.join(__dirname, 'board.json');
const tag = { purpose: 'setup' };
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));

async function main() {
  const n = Number(process.argv[2] || 32);
  const b = fs.existsSync(OUT) ? JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(OUT, 'utf8')) : { repo: REPO, items: {} };
  const save = () => fs.writeFileSync(OUT, JSON.stringify(b, null, 2));
  if (!b.repoNodeId) {
    const r = await rest('POST', '/user/repos', { name: REPO, private: true, auto_init: true,
      description: 'Scratch repository for rigger #1431 (throwaway spike). Owner deletes by hand.' }, tag);
    Object.assign(b, { repoUrl: r.html_url, repoNodeId: r.node_id, repoPrivate: r.private }); save();
  }
  if (!b.projectId) {
    const v = await graphql('Viewer', 'query Viewer { viewer { id login } rateLimit { cost remaining used } }', {}, tag);
    const d = await graphql('CreateProject', `mutation CreateProject($o: ID!, $r: ID!) {
      createProjectV2(input: { ownerId: $o, title: "rigger-1431 mirror scratch", repositoryId: $r }) {
        projectV2 { id number url owner { __typename } } } }`, { o: v.viewer.id, r: b.repoNodeId }, tag);
    const p = d.createProjectV2.projectV2;
    Object.assign(b, { projectId: p.id, projectNumber: p.number, projectUrl: p.url, projectOwnerType: p.owner.__typename }); save();
  }
  if (!b.statusFieldId) {
    const d = await graphql('ReadFields', `query ReadFields($p: ID!) { node(id: $p) { ... on ProjectV2 {
      field(name: "Status") { ... on ProjectV2SingleSelectField { id options { id name } } } } }
      rateLimit { cost remaining used } }`, { p: b.projectId }, tag);
    const f = d.node.field;
    const u = await graphql('SetStatusOptions', `mutation SetStatusOptions($f: ID!, $o: [ProjectV2SingleSelectFieldOptionInput!]) {
      updateProjectV2Field(input: { fieldId: $f, singleSelectOptions: $o }) {
        projectV2Field { ... on ProjectV2SingleSelectField { id options { id name } } } } }`,
      { f: f.id, o: COLUMNS.map((name, i) => ({ name, color: COLORS[i], description: '' })) }, tag);
    const sf = u.updateProjectV2Field.projectV2Field;
    b.statusFieldId = sf.id;
    b.statusOptions = Object.fromEntries(sf.options.map((o) => [o.name, o.id])); save();
  }
  if (!b.escalationFieldId) {
    const d = await graphql('CreateEscalationField', `mutation CreateEscalationField($p: ID!) {
      createProjectV2Field(input: { projectId: $p, dataType: TEXT, name: "Escalation" }) {
        projectV2Field { ... on ProjectV2Field { id } } } }`, { p: b.projectId }, tag);
    b.escalationFieldId = d.createProjectV2Field.projectV2Field.id; save();
  }
  for (let i = Object.keys(b.items).length + 1; i <= n; i += 1) {
    const issue = await rest('POST', `/repos/${OWNER}/${REPO}/issues`, { title: `Scratch card ${i}` }, tag);
    const d = await graphql('AddItem', `mutation AddItem($p: ID!, $c: ID!) {
      addProjectV2ItemById(input: { projectId: $p, contentId: $c }) { item { id } } }`,
      { p: b.projectId, c: issue.node_id }, tag);
    b.items[issue.number] = d.addProjectV2ItemById.item.id; save();
    await sleep(1500);
  }
  console.log(JSON.stringify({ repoUrl: b.repoUrl, repoPrivate: b.repoPrivate, projectUrl: b.projectUrl,
    projectNumber: b.projectNumber, projectOwnerType: b.projectOwnerType, items: Object.keys(b.items).length }));
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF
node --check setup.js && for f in forge board activities workflows worker start edit watch scan; do node --check $f.js || echo BAD $f; done; echo checked

=== [15] issued 2026-11-19T16:00:28.594Z  answered 2026-11-19T16:01:54.530Z  Bash
description: Log failed fetches, make setup resumable, rerun  [timeout=300000]
S=~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer; cd $S && python3 - <<'EOF'
p='forge.js'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("""  const res = await fetch('https://api.github.com' + endpoint, {""","""  const res = await guarded(() => fetch('https://api.github.com' + endpoint, {""")
s=s.replace("""    body: body ? JSON.stringify(body) : undefined,
  });""","""    body: body ? JSON.stringify(body) : undefined,
  }), { ts: t0.toISOString(), api: 'rest', rw: method === 'GET' ? 'read' : 'write', method, endpoint, ...tag });""")
s=s.replace("""  const res = await fetch('https://api.github.com/graphql', {""","""  const res = await guarded(() => fetch('https://api.github.com/graphql', {""")
s=s.replace("""    body: JSON.stringify({ query, variables }),
  });""","""    body: JSON.stringify({ query, variables }),
  }), { ts: t0.toISOString(), api: 'graphql', rw: /^\\s*mutation/.test(query) ? 'write' : 'read', method: 'POST', operation: op, ...tag });""")
s=s.replace("""function log(entry) { fs.appendFileSync(LOG, JSON.stringify(entry) + '\\n'); }""","""function log(entry) { fs.appendFileSync(LOG, JSON.stringify(entry) + '\\n'); }

// A call that fails below HTTP (DNS, TLS, reset) is still a call that may have landed: log it.
async function guarded(send, entry) {
  try { return await send(); } catch (e) {
    log({ ...entry, status: 'network-error', error: String(e.cause?.code || e.message) });
    throw e;
  }
}""")
open(p,'w').write(s)
p='setup.js'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("""    const issue = await rest('POST', `/repos/${OWNER}/${REPO}/issues`, { title: `Scratch card ${i}` }, tag);""",
"""    // A run cut off after creating issue i but before adding it resumes on that issue.
    const issue = await rest('GET', `/repos/${OWNER}/${REPO}/issues/${i}`, null, tag).catch(() => null)
      || await rest('POST', `/repos/${OWNER}/${REPO}/issues`, { title: `Scratch card ${i}` }, tag);""")
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

=== [17] issued 2026-11-19T16:02:39.563Z  answered 2026-11-19T16:02:39.737Z  Bash
description: Write remote wrapper and run plan
S=~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer; cd $S && cat > r.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ABOUTME: Runs one command on the remote spike host in ~/1431-spike-engineer, with GITHUB_TOKEN
# read from stdin (never a file or argv there), logged through t.sh. Usage: HOST=<h> r.sh '<cmd>'
D="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
: "${HOST:?set HOST}"
exec "$D/t.sh" "gh auth token | ssh -o UserKnownHostsFile=$D/known_hosts $HOST 'cd ~/1431-spike-engineer && read -r GITHUB_TOKEN && export GITHUB_TOKEN TEMPORAL_ADDRESS=localhost:7243 && $1'"
EOF
chmod +x r.sh; cat > run-plan.md <<'EOF'
# Spike 1431 run plan (throwaway)

Host: the WSL2 machine over SSH (bash). The Windows machine is the fallback.
All forge calls, the server, the worker, the temporal CLI and the watchers run on that one host, so
every timestamp comes from one clock. Reconcile interval R = 30 s. Read-back delay D = 3 s.
The revert bound is 2R = 60 s: the next reconcile timer fires within R, and one mirror run takes
well under R. watch.js polls every 1 s, with a 180 s timeout.

## Deploy
1. `ssh HOST 'mkdir -p ~/1431-spike-engineer'`, then rsync the code and board.json, without node_modules or the logs.
2. Check `node -v` (needs >=20.3). If it is missing, get the Node tarball into the dir.
3. Download the Temporal CLI release into the dir. Run `./temporal --version`.
4. `npm install` (approve the core-bridge install scripts if npm 11 blocks them), then `npm ls`.
5. Start the server: `setsid nohup ./temporal server start-dev --port 7243 --ui-port 8243 --db-filename ~/1431-spike-engineer/temporal.db > server.log 2>&1 < /dev/null & echo $!` and record the pid.
6. Start the worker through r.sh: `setsid nohup node worker.js > worker.log 2>&1 < /dev/null & echo $!` and record the pid. The token is in the env, read from stdin.

## Runs
| Run | Card | Escalate in | Steps |
|---|---|---|---|
| L (lifecycle + escalation + I1 drift) | 1 | Coding | start → advance (Coding, escalates) → `temporal workflow signal --name answer` and watch for Review (time from signal) → while held in Review: edit.js 1 Done (I1), watch for Review (time from the edit) → advance ×2 → Done |
| I2a (edit between the read and the write, reconcile run) | 2 | - | held in Ready; `touch gates/2.after-read`; wait for `gates/2.after-read.held`; edit.js 2 Review; `touch gates/2.release`; readback, then the next reconcile reverts |
| I2b (same, transition run) | 2 | - | `touch gates/2.after-read`; advance → the read sees Ready and holds; edit.js 2 Done; release; the mirror writes Coding |
| I3 | 3 | - | `touch gates/3.after-writes`; advance → it writes Coding and holds; edit.js 3 Owner; release; readback; the next reconcile reverts |
| I4 | 4 | Coding | advance → Coding + escalation; `touch gates/4.after-write1`; signal answer → it writes Status=Review and holds; `kill -9 <worker pid>`; start a new worker and record its pid; a heartbeat timeout of 10 s → attempt 2 writes only Escalation |
| Twice-run | 5 | - | the reconcile runs on a card held in Ready, with no change between: the writes in the 2nd run come from calllog |
| 30-card pass | 2–32 (31 running) | - | start 5..32; once all have settled, count the mirror-read and mirror-write calls with why=reconcile in one R window; plus one `node scan.js` for the batched route |
| Event history | all | - | `temporal workflow show -w '<repo>#<n>' --output json` → evidence |

## Teardown
Kill the worker and server pids I recorded. Take a process listing. Copy back the call log, mirror-runs, histories and logs. Delete the board (deleteProjectV2) and read its node id back to show not-found. The repository is left for the owner.
EOF
echo ok

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ABOUTME: Runs one cmd.exe command on the Windows spike host (hostpc) in its spike directory,
# logged through t.sh. With TOKEN=1 the gh token goes to the command's stdin. Usage: [TOKEN=1] w.sh '<cmd>'
D="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
SSH="ssh -o UserKnownHostsFile=$D/known_hosts hostpc"
if [ -n "$TOKEN" ]; then
  exec "$D/t.sh" "gh auth token | $SSH 'cd /d %USERPROFILE%\\1431-spike-engineer && $1'"
else
  exec "$D/t.sh" "$SSH 'cd /d %USERPROFILE%\\1431-spike-engineer && $1' < /dev/null"
fi
EOF
chmod +x w.sh
./t.sh "ssh -o UserKnownHostsFile=$S/known_hosts hostpc 'mkdir %USERPROFILE%\\1431-spike-engineer' < /dev/null && cd $S && scp -o UserKnownHostsFile=$S/known_hosts package.json package-lock.json forge.js board.js activities.js workflows.js worker.js start.js edit.js watch.js scan.js board.json hostpc:1431-spike-engineer/"
./w.sh 'dir /b'

=== [26] issued 2026-11-20T00:41:32.686Z  answered 2026-11-20T00:41:32.930Z  Bash
description: Write held-session launcher
S=~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer; cd $S && cat > hold.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ABOUTME: Holds one long-running cmd.exe command open on hostpc in an ssh session from the laptop,
# recording the laptop ssh pid in pids.log and the transcript. Usage: [TOKEN=1] hold.sh <name> '<cmd>'
D="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"; name=$1; cmd=$2
SSH=(ssh -o UserKnownHostsFile="$D/known_hosts" -o ServerAliveInterval=30 hostpc "cd /d %USERPROFILE%\\1431-spike-engineer && $cmd")
if [ -n "$TOKEN" ]; then gh auth token | "${SSH[@]}" & else "${SSH[@]}" < /dev/null & fi
pid=$!; ts=$(date -u +%FT%TZ)
echo "$ts laptop ssh pid=$pid holds $name: $cmd" >> "$D/pids.log"
printf '\n### %s `%s`\n\n```\n$ [held, background] %s ssh hostpc "%s"\nlaptop ssh pid=%s\n```\n' "$ts" "$(hostname -s)" "${TOKEN:+gh auth token |}" "$cmd" "$pid" >> "$D/transcript.md"
wait $pid; rc=$?
echo "$(date -u +%FT%TZ) laptop ssh pid=$pid ($name) exited rc=$rc" | tee -a "$D/pids.log" >> "$D/transcript.md"
EOF
chmod +x hold.sh; echo ok

=== [40] issued 2026-11-20T00:42:43.462Z  answered 2026-11-20T00:42:44.452Z  Bash
description: Add signal-and-watch helper, copy to Windows
S=~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer; cd $S && cat > sigwatch.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Sends a signal through the Temporal CLI, noting the instant just before, then polls the
// card's board item every 500 ms until it shows the target column, and prints the elapsed time.
// Usage: node sigwatch.js <card> <signal> <targetColumn> [inputJson]
const { execFileSync } = require('child_process');
const { readItem, board } = require('./board');
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
async function main() {
  const [card, signal, column, input] = process.argv.slice(2);
  const args = ['workflow', 'signal', '--address', 'localhost:7243', '--workflow-id', `${board().repo}#${card}`, '--name', signal];
  if (input) args.push('--input', input);
  console.log('command: temporal.exe ' + args.map((a) => (/[\s"]/.test(a) ? `'${a}'` : a)).join(' '));
  const t0 = Date.now();
  console.log('signal sent at', new Date(t0).toISOString());
  console.log(execFileSync('temporal.exe', args, { encoding: 'utf8' }).trim());
  for (;;) {
    const at = Date.now();
    const s = await readItem(card, { purpose: 'measure' });
    if (s.column === column) { console.log(JSON.stringify({ card, column, seenAt: new Date(at).toISOString(), elapsedMs: at - t0, state: s })); return; }
    if (at - t0 > 180000) { console.log(JSON.stringify({ timedOut: true, s })); process.exitCode = 2; return; }
    await sleep(500);
  }
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF
sed -i '' "s/intervalMs = '1000'/intervalMs = '500'/" watch.js
./t.sh "scp -o UserKnownHostsFile=$S/known_hosts $S/sigwatch.js $S/watch.js hostpc:1431-spike-engineer/"
./w.sh 'type mirror-runs.jsonl' | grep '"end"' | grep -E '"card":"1"' | tail -1 | cut -c1-300

=== [42] issued 2026-11-20T00:42:59.497Z  answered 2026-11-20T00:43:24.904Z  Bash
description: I1: edit card 1 to Done with no run in flight, time revert  [timeout=120000]
S=~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer; cd $S && cat > editwatch.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Plays the person: moves a card's board item to another column through the API, then
// polls every 500 ms until the board shows the workflow's column again, timing it from the edit's
// answer. Usage: node editwatch.js <card> <editColumn> <workflowColumn> [timeoutMs]
const { setColumn, readItem } = require('./board');
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
async function main() {
  const [card, editCol, wfCol, timeoutMs = '120000'] = process.argv.slice(2);
  await setColumn(card, editCol, { purpose: 'edit-drift' });
  const t0 = Date.now();
  console.log('edit answered at', new Date(t0).toISOString(), card, '->', editCol);
  for (;;) {
    const at = Date.now();
    const s = await readItem(card, { purpose: 'measure' });
    if (s.column === wfCol) { console.log(JSON.stringify({ card, revertedTo: wfCol, seenAt: new Date(at).toISOString(), elapsedMs: at - t0, state: s })); return; }
    if (at - t0 > Number(timeoutMs)) { console.log(JSON.stringify({ card, timedOut: true, boundMs: Number(timeoutMs), lastState: s })); process.exitCode = 2; return; }
    await sleep(500);
  }
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF
./t.sh "scp -q -o UserKnownHostsFile=$S/known_hosts $S/editwatch.js hostpc:1431-spike-engineer/"

=== [44] issued 2026-11-20T00:43:47.480Z  answered 2026-11-20T00:43:50.289Z  Bash
description: I2a: edit card 2 between reconcile read and write  [timeout=120000]
S=~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer; cd $S && cat > gated.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Places a person's edit at a gate inside one mirrorCard run: arms gates/<card>.<point>,
// optionally sends an advance signal, waits until the run holds at the gate, edits the column
// through the API, then releases the run. Usage: node gated.js <card> <point> <editColumn> [advance]
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { execFileSync } = require('child_process');
const { setColumn, board } = require('./board');
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
const say = (...a) => console.log(new Date().toISOString(), ...a);
async function main() {
  const [card, point, editCol, adv] = process.argv.slice(2);
  const G = path.join(__dirname, 'gates');
  fs.mkdirSync(G, { recursive: true });
  fs.writeFileSync(path.join(G, `${card}.${point}`), '');
  say('armed', `gates/${card}.${point}`);
  if (adv) { execFileSync('temporal.exe', ['workflow', 'signal', '--address', 'localhost:7243', '--workflow-id', `${board().repo}#${card}`, '--name', 'advance']); say('sent advance'); }
  while (!fs.existsSync(path.join(G, `${card}.${point}.held`))) await sleep(100);
  say('run holds at', point);
  if (editCol !== '-') { await setColumn(card, editCol, { purpose: 'edit-drift' }); say('edit answered', card, '->', editCol); }
  fs.writeFileSync(path.join(G, `${card}.release`), '');
  say('released');
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF
./t.sh "scp -q -o UserKnownHostsFile=$S/known_hosts $S/gated.js hostpc:1431-spike-engineer/"
TOKEN=1 ./w.sh 'node gated.js 2 after-read Review'

=== [74] issued 2026-11-20T00:48:11.104Z  answered 2026-11-20T00:48:12.974Z  Bash
description: Delete scratch board and read back not-found
S=~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer; cd $S && cat > teardown.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Deletes spike 1431's scratch board with deleteProjectV2, then reads its node id and its
// number back and prints the forge's answers.
const { graphql } = require('./forge');
const b = require('./board.json');
async function main() {
  const d = await graphql('DeleteProject', `mutation DeleteProject($p: ID!) { deleteProjectV2(input: { projectId: $p }) { projectV2 { id number } } }`, { p: b.projectId }, { purpose: 'teardown' });
  console.log('deleted', JSON.stringify(d));
  for (const [op, q, v] of [
    ['ReadDeletedNode', 'query ReadDeletedNode($p: ID!) { node(id: $p) { id } rateLimit { cost } }', { p: b.projectId }],
    ['ReadDeletedNumber', 'query ReadDeletedNumber($n: Int!) { user(login: "williacj") { projectV2(number: $n) { id } } rateLimit { cost } }', { n: b.projectNumber }],
  ]) {
    const res = await fetch('https://api.github.com/graphql', { method: 'POST', headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN}`, 'content-type': 'application/json', 'user-agent': 'spike-1431' }, body: JSON.stringify({ query: q, variables: v }) });
    const text = await res.text();
    require('./forge').log({ ts: new Date().toISOString(), api: 'graphql', rw: 'read', method: 'POST', operation: op, status: res.status, purpose: 'teardown' });
    console.log(op, res.status, text);
  }
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF
./t.sh "cd $S && GITHUB_TOKEN=\$(gh auth token) node teardown.js"

=== [76] issued 2026-11-20T00:48:33.531Z  answered 2026-11-20T00:48:33.742Z  Bash
description: Analyse call logs for card 1 and card 5
S=~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer; cd $S && cat > analyse.py <<'EOF'
# ABOUTME: Throwaway analysis of spike 1431's call logs and mirror runs.
import json, collections
L=[json.loads(l) for l in open('calllog.jsonl')]; R=[json.loads(l) for l in open('remote/calllog.jsonl')]
for e in L: e['host']='laptop'
for e in R: e['host']='hostpc'
A=sorted(L+R,key=lambda e:e['ts'])
print('total calls',len(A), collections.Counter((e['host'],e.get('purpose')) for e in A))
c1=[e for e in R if e.get('card')=='1']
print('\ncard1 by purpose/api/rw', collections.Counter((e['purpose'],e['api'],e['rw']) for e in c1))
prod=[e for e in c1 if e['purpose'] in('mirror-read','mirror-write')]
print('card1 production', len(prod), collections.Counter((e['api'],e['rw'],e.get('why')) for e in prod))
print('card1 query cost sum (production reads)', sum(e['cost'] or 0 for e in prod if e['rw']=='read'))
print('card1 cost sum all reads', sum(e['cost'] or 0 for e in c1 if e['rw']=='read'))
print('first/last card1', c1[0]['ts'], c1[-1]['ts'])
# used deltas around mutations
print('\nsample graphql used sequence (card1 production):')
for e in prod: print(e['ts'][11:23], e['operation'], e.get('why'), 'used', e['ratelimit']['used'], 'cost', e['cost'])
# card 5 runs
print('\ncard5:')
for e in R:
  if e.get('card')=='5' and e['purpose'] in ('mirror-read','mirror-write'): print(e['ts'][11:23], e['operation'], e.get('why'))
EOF
python3 analyse.py 2>&1 | head -80

=== [77] issued 2026-11-20T00:48:43.730Z  answered 2026-11-20T00:48:43.956Z  Bash
description: Group GraphQL rate-limit answers by reset window
S=~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer; cd $S && python3 - <<'EOF'
import json,collections
R=[json.loads(l) for l in open('remote/calllog.jsonl')]+[json.loads(l) for l in open('calllog.jsonl')]
R.sort(key=lambda e:e['ts'])
g=[e for e in R if e['api']=='graphql' and e.get('ratelimit')]
print(collections.Counter(e['ratelimit']['reset'] for e in g))
# within each reset bucket, used deltas between consecutive calls by op type
for reset in set(e['ratelimit']['reset'] for e in g):
  b=[e for e in g if e['ratelimit']['reset']==reset]
  d=collections.defaultdict(list)
  for p,c in zip(b,b[1:]):
    d[c['rw']].append(int(c['ratelimit']['used'])-int(p['ratelimit']['used']))
  print(reset, len(b), {k:collections.Counter(v).most_common(6) for k,v in d.items()})
EOF

=== [79] issued 2026-11-20T00:49:04.794Z  answered 2026-11-20T00:49:05.105Z  Bash
description: Extract signal and retry events from histories
S=~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer; cd $S && python3 - <<'EOF'
import json,collections,base64
for n in [1,2,3,4]:
  h=json.load(open(f'remote/hist/card-{n}.json'))
  ev=h['events']
  print('card',n,len(ev),collections.Counter(e['eventType'] for e in ev))
  for e in ev:
    if 'Signaled' in e['eventType']:
      a=e['workflowExecutionSignaledEventAttributes']
      inp=a.get('input',{}).get('payloads',[{}])
      data=[base64.b64decode(p['data']).decode() for p in inp if 'data' in p]
      print('  ',e['eventId'],e['eventTime'],e['eventType'],a['signalName'],data)
    if e['eventType']=='EVENT_TYPE_ACTIVITY_TASK_STARTED' and int(e['workflowActivityTaskStartedEventAttributes' if False else 'eventId'])>0:
      a=e['activityTaskStartedEventAttributes']
      if a.get('attempt',1)>1: print('  retry',e['eventId'],e['eventTime'],'attempt',a['attempt'], a.get('lastFailure',{}).get('message'))
EOF

=== [104] issued 2026-11-20T00:55:25.224Z  answered 2026-11-20T00:55:25.359Z  Write
file_path: /Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror.md

<!-- ABOUTME: Spike findings for card #1431: a Projects v2 board as a one-way mirror of Temporal card
workflows. Drift reconcile, signal-borne answers, the four edit/mirror interleavings, and forge cost. -->

# A Projects v2 board as a one-way mirror of Temporal card workflows

Card [#1431](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1431), acceptance revision `3c509245f0e7`.
That revision fills in the hosts and leaves the acceptance as it stood at `cb6b5a7391b7`. Every
figure below is a **measurement** unless it is marked as a judgment. Each measurement names the
evidence file it comes from, and those files sit in [`projects-v2-mirror/`](projects-v2-mirror/)
beside this report.

## Answer

The board converged back to the workflow every time.
- **Drift:** after an API edit, the per-card reconcile restored the workflow's column in 22.6 s
  (I1), 35.0 s (I2) and 35.0 s (I3), against a stated bound of 60 s.
- **Escalation:** the answer, sent as a signal from the Temporal CLI, moved the board to the next
  column 1.3 s after the signal was sent.
- **Kill mid-write (I4):** killing the worker between the two field writes of one run left a
  partial board. The retried activity found it and wrote only the field still missing.
- **The workflow's own state never changed** because of a board edit.
- **Cost of one card:** from Ready to Done, with one escalation and one drift, it made **15
  production forge calls**, all GraphQL: 7 reads and 8 writes.

On affordability, the mirror is affordable at the card's load **if the reconcile reads the board
in one batched query per pass**. That costs about 120 GraphQL points an hour at a 30 s interval.
It is **not affordable on a shared user token as I ran it**, with one read per card per pass. At
30 open cards that costs about 3,600 points an hour, 72% of the user's 5,000-point hour.

## What I ran

### Host and versions

The server, the worker, every forge call during the runs and every timing ran on the Windows
machine. The laptop held the SSH sessions open and made the setup and teardown calls. Each value
below is quoted from the command that printed it, in [`transcript.md`](projects-v2-mirror/transcript.md)
at 2026-11-20T00:46Z, plus 00:40Z for `--version`.

| What | Value, as printed |
|---|---|
| Host | `Host-PC10` (`ssh hostpc`, `user@10.0.0.29` port 2223, Windows OpenSSH, `cmd.exe`) |
| OS and version | `Microsoft Windows [Version 10.0.26300.9974]`, `AMD64` |
| Node | `v24.19.0` (npm `11.17.0`) |
| Temporal CLI | `temporal version 1.10.1 (Server 1.32.2, UI 2.56.4)` |
| `@temporalio/*` (every one, from `npm ls --all --parseable --long`) | `activity@1.26.0`, `client@1.26.0`, `worker@1.26.0`, `workflow@1.26.0`, `common@1.26.0`, `proto@1.26.0`, `core-bridge@1.26.0`, `nexus@1.26.0` |
| Laptop (setup, teardown, SSH client) | `[laptop host]`, macOS `27.2.1`, Node `v26.13.0` |

**Why Windows and not WSL2.** WSL2 is the card's other primary host. At 00:40:08Z
`ssh wsl-hostpc` answered `kex_exchange_identification: read: Connection reset by peer`, and
rigger-ae's direction was to use `hostpc` in that case. The stated-intent addendum records the
switch.

**How it ran.**
- `temporal.exe server start-dev --port 7243 --ui-port 8243 --db-filename C:\Users\USER\1431-spike-engineer\temporal.db --log-level warn`
  ran with one worker on task queue `spike-1431`.
- Windows OpenSSH ends a session's children when the session closes. The laptop therefore held
  each long-running process open in its own `ssh` session.

### Forge, token and comments

- **Forge plan, posted first:** before any forge object existed, I posted
  [1431#7014271069](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1431#issuecomment-7014271069). It
  names the scratch repository and board, the credential's scopes and how the board is deleted.
  It is also the stated-intent comment, posted before the first install, the laptop
  `npm install` at 15:53Z.
- **Addendum, before the first remote install:**
  [1431#7026615458](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1431#issuecomment-7026615458),
  posted before the Temporal CLI download on hostpc.
- **Scratch repository:** <https://github.com/williacj/rigger-1431-mirror-scratch>.
  - Its visibility is **private**: `repoPrivate: true` from the create call, and
    `gh repo view … visibility` answered `PRIVATE`.
  - It holds 32 issues, "Scratch card 1..32".
- **Scratch board:** <https://github.com/users/williacj/projects/14>, number 14, node
  `PVT_kwHOBzomGc4BsXT-`.
  - Its owner is a **user**, `williacj`: the create call answered `owner { __typename }` =
    `User`.
  - Its Status field has the options Ready, Coding, Review, Owner and Done. Those are the names
    in `rigger.config.mjs` at `6be13718`, line 17. It also has an `Escalation` text field.
- **Credential:** the `gh` CLI's OAuth token for `williacj`. I stated its scopes in the comment
  before the first call: `delete_repo`, `gist`, `project`, `read:org`, `repo` and `workflow`. The
  call log holds no call that needs `delete_repo`, `gist` or `workflow`. On hostpc the token
  reached each Node process on stdin and was never written to a file there.

### The code (throwaway, not committed)

It lived in the gitignored `spikes/1431-spike-engineer/`, and was copied to
`C:\Users\USER\1431-spike-engineer\`, which I have since removed.

- **`cardWorkflow`:**
  - Its workflow id is the card, `rigger-1431-mirror-scratch#<n>`.
  - It holds `{column, escalation}`, and an `advance` signal moves it on one column.
  - On entering `Coding`, cards 1 and 4 escalate: escalation becomes
    `ambiguous: waiting on owner answer`. They then wait for an `answer` signal, which clears the
    escalation and moves the card to Review.
  - While it waits it runs `wf.condition(…, 30 s)`, and on every timeout it runs `mirrorCard`
    again. That re-run is the reconcile.
- **`mirrorCard`:**
  - It reads the item's Status and Escalation in one GraphQL query.
  - It writes each field that differs from the workflow's full state: `SetStatus`, then
    `SetEscalation` or `ClearEscalation`.
  - It waits the read-back delay, then reads the item again. That second read is instrumentation,
    not part of the mirror.
  - Running it twice with no change writes nothing the second time; see the run-twice check.
  - File gates let me hold one run after its read, after its first write, or after its writes.
  - Activity options: `startToCloseTimeout` 2 min, `heartbeatTimeout` 10 s, retry interval 1 s.
- **Call logger:** every forge call went through one logger. It recorded the time, REST or
  GraphQL, read or write, the method, the endpoint or operation, the HTTP status, the
  `x-ratelimit-*` headers and the GraphQL `rateLimit.cost`. That is
  [`calllog.json`](projects-v2-mirror/calllog.json): 599 calls, merged from both hosts.

### Read-back delay

**Each `mirrorCard` waited 3,000 ms between its last write and its read-back from the forge**
(`READBACK_DELAY_MS`, recorded as `readbackDelayMs: 3000` in every `end` event in
[`mirror-runs.json`](projects-v2-mirror/mirror-runs.json)). The watchers that timed the signal
and the drift polled the item every 500 ms.

### Drift detection

- **The route I ran:** every card workflow polls its own board item. On every reconcile timeout
  the workflow runs `mirrorCard`, which reads the item and writes back any field that differs.
- **Reconcile interval:** 30,000 ms (`node start.js 30000 …`). The timer starts when the previous
  run ends. A run takes about 3.5 s with the read-back, so the measured cadence per card was about
  33.5 s (card 5's reconcile ends: 00:42:45.7, 00:43:19.3, 00:43:53.2, …).
- **Bound:** 60 s, which is twice the interval. An edit is reverted by the first reconcile run
  that starts after it. That run starts at most 30 s plus one run's length after the edit, and
  its write lands within one more run's length. With a run of about 3.5 s, everything fits
  within 2 × 30 s.
- **How the board was changed:** every drift edit went **through the API**
  (`updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue`, purpose `edit-drift` in the call log), never through the web
  interface.

**Routes I considered and did not run.**

| Route | Why not |
|---|---|
| A `projects_v2_item` webhook | The GitHub docs list its availability as `organization` only. They describe it as "activity relating to an item on an organization-level project", and this board is user-owned. The card also puts internet-delivered webhooks out of scope. |
| A GitHub Actions workflow triggered by a board edit | The Actions page "Events that trigger workflows", read on 2026-11-20, lists no Projects v2 event or `project` trigger. |
| One batched board read per pass (`ScanBoard`, 100 items a page), mapping drift to card workflows | It needs a coordinating workflow, or an activity that reaches card workflows through the client. That is machinery beyond the card's "the workflow's reconcile". I **measured its cost instead**: one `ScanBoard` over the 32-item board was one call with `rateLimit.cost` 1 (call log, 00:46:28.487Z, purpose `batched-reconcile-pass`). I did not time drift reverts with it. |
| A blind full-state write every interval, with no read | It writes forever. That is 2 mutations per card per pass, each costing 5 secondary-limit points, where read-compare costs 1 query and no write when nothing has drifted. |

## Findings

### Card 1, Ready to Done, with its escalation and one drift

Only card 1 was moved all the way from Ready to Done. The table gives the board's column after
each `mirrorCard` run, read back from the forge 3 s after the run's writes, against the column
the run carried. Source: `end` events for card 1 in `mirror-runs.json`.

| Run ended (UTC) | Why | Writes | Board read back | Workflow column | Match |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 00:42:10.858 | transition | Status | Ready | Ready | yes |
| 00:42:27.052 | transition | Status | Coding | Coding | yes |
| 00:42:31.503 | transition (escalate) | Escalation | Coding, escalation set | Coding, escalation set | yes |
| 00:42:52.807 | transition (answer) | Status, Escalation | Review | Review | yes |
| 00:43:27.756 | reconcile (I1 drift) | Status | Review | Review | yes |
| 00:43:35.379 | transition | Status | Owner | Owner | yes |
| 00:43:39.886 | transition | Status | Done | Done | yes |

### Running `mirrorCard` twice with no change

Card 5 was held in Ready for its whole run.
- Its first run, at 00:42:06Z, made one write (`SetStatus`).
- Every later run made **0 forge writes**. That is nine reconcile runs from 00:42:42 to 00:47:21,
  each a single `ReadItem` with no write after it. Source: the call log entries for card 5;
  `end` events with `writes: []` in `mirror-runs.json`.

### Escalation answered by a signal

The command that sent the answer was run on hostpc by `sigwatch.js` and printed by it; see the
transcript entry at 2026-11-20T00:42:48Z:

```
temporal.exe workflow signal --address localhost:7243 --workflow-id rigger-1431-mirror-scratch#1 --name answer --input '"proceed: owner answer"'
```

- **The workflow received it:** [`histories/card-1.json`](projects-v2-mirror/histories/card-1.json)
  event 30 is `EVENT_TYPE_WORKFLOW_EXECUTION_SIGNALED` at `2026-11-20T00:42:48.197152700Z`, with
  `signalName` `answer`, input `"proceed: owner answer"` and identity
  `temporal-cli:HOST-PC10\USER@Host-PC10`. Event 35 schedules `mirrorCard` with
  `{column: Review, escalation: ''}`.
- **Signal to board:** the signal was sent at 00:42:48.146Z. The board was first seen showing
  Review at 00:42:49.442Z, so **1,296 ms** elapsed (`sigwatch.js` output, 500 ms polling).

### Drift

| Drift | Card and edit | Edit answered | Board back to the workflow's column | Measured time | Reverted within 60 s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| I1 | 1: Review → Done | 00:43:01.675Z | 00:43:24.262Z | **22,587 ms** | yes |
| I2a | 2: Ready → Review | 00:43:49.893Z | 00:44:24.847Z | **34,954 ms** | yes |
| I3 | 3: Coding → Owner | 00:44:41.493Z | 00:45:16.530Z | **35,037 ms** | yes |

**The workflow's own state did not change.** In each case its history shows no signal during the
drift, and the next activity carries the unchanged state.

| Card | Drift window | Signals | Next activity carries | Other evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | between the `answer` signal (event 30) and the next `advance` (event 53) | none | event 46, `reconcile` with `{Review, ''}` | — |
| 2 | 00:43:49–00:44:24 | none (its only signal is event 56 at 00:44:31) | events 38 and 49, `reconcile` with `{Ready, ''}` | event 40's result reads back `Review` with `match: false`, and the next activity still carries `Ready` |
| 3 | from the edit to the revert | none | event 72, `reconcile` with `{Coding, ''}` | `temporal workflow query --name state` answered `{"column":"Coding","escalation":""}` for cards 2 and 3 at 00:46:54Z |

### The interleavings

The final state of each row was read back from the forge, by the run's read-back, a watcher or a
`scan.js` read.

| Row | What happened | Board's final state | Equals the workflow's? |
|---|---|---|---|
| I1 | Card 1's edit landed at 00:43:01.675Z. The previous run ended at 00:42:52.807 and the next started at 00:43:22.8, so no run was in flight. The reconcile read `Done` and wrote `Review`. | `{Review, ''}`: read back at 00:43:27.756 | **yes** |
| I2, reconcile run (I2a) | Card 2 was held in Ready. The run read `Ready`, which matched, and was held after its read while the edit to `Review` landed. Released, it wrote nothing, because its read had matched. | `{Review, ''}` at the run's read-back (00:43:53.393) | **no**. The next reconcile, at 00:44:24.590, wrote Ready; read back `{Ready, ''}`, which **equals** the workflow. |
| I2, transition run (I2b) | Card 2's `advance` started a run that read `Ready`. It was held after its read while the edit to `Done` landed. Released, it wrote `Coding`. | `{Coding, ''}`: read-back at 00:44:37.136, and a `scan.js` read just after | **yes** |
| I3 | Card 3's transition run wrote `Coding` and was held after its writes while the edit to `Owner` landed. | `{Owner, ''}` at the run's read-back (00:44:44.852) | **no**. The next reconcile wrote Coding at 00:45:16; read back `{Coding, ''}`, which **equals** the workflow. |
| I4 | Card 4 was escalated in Coding; the answer started a run. It wrote `Status=Review`, was held after that first write, and its worker (pid 34000) was killed with `taskkill /F`. The forge then read `{Review, 'ambiguous: waiting on owner answer'}`, a partial write. A new worker (pid 30548) took the retry. History event 91 is attempt 2, with last failure `activity Heartbeat timeout`, at 00:45:37.6Z, 13 s after the kill. Attempt 2 read the partial state and wrote **only** `ClearEscalation`. | `{Review, ''}`: read back at 00:45:42.487, and by `scan.js` | **yes** |

**How I placed the I2 edit between the read and the write.**
1. `node gated.js 2 after-read Review` (transcript entry 2026-11-20T00:43:48Z) created
   `gates/2.after-read`.
2. The next `mirrorCard` run for card 2 did its read, found the file, renamed it to `.held`, and
   waited, heartbeating, for `gates/2.release`. `mirror-runs.json` records `read` at 00:43:48.927
   and `gate-held` at 00:43:48.928.
3. `gated.js` saw the `.held` file and sent the edit; its answer came at 00:43:49.893.
4. `gated.js` then created the release file, and the run went on (`gate-released`,
   00:43:49.956).

I2b used the same steps; see the transcript entry at 00:44:31Z.

**What I2a shows.** A read-compare-write mirror cannot see an edit that lands after its read in a
run that found nothing to write. The run then reports success over a drifted board, and the drift
lasts until the next reconcile. That is bounded by the 60 s bound, and it measured 35 s here.

### Forge calls for one card

These are card 1's calls from Ready to Done (00:42:06–00:43:39Z), with its escalation and the I1
drift. Source: entries with `card: "1"` in the call log.

| | REST | GraphQL reads | GraphQL writes | Total |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Production (`mirror-read`, `mirror-write`) | 0 | 7 (`ReadItem`) | 8 (6 `SetStatus` transitions, 1 `SetEscalation`, 1 `ClearEscalation`; plus 1 `SetStatus` from the reconcile, which is counted in the 6 + 1 + 1 … see the note) | **15** |
| Instrumentation (`readback`, `measure`, `edit-drift`) | 0 | 49 (7 read-backs, 42 watcher polls) | 1 (the drift edit) | 50 |

Note on the 8 writes, by operation: `SetStatus` ×6, of which 5 were transitions and 1 was the
reconcile's revert, plus `SetEscalation` ×1 and `ClearEscalation` ×1. By reason: 7 were written
during transition runs and 1 during the reconcile. The reads split the same way: 6 transition
and 1 reconcile.

The signals and the workflow's state cost no forge call. They are Temporal-only.

**GraphQL rate-limit cost of that lifecycle.**
- **Queries:** the sum of the forge's `rateLimit.cost` answers over the 7 production queries is
  **7 points**. Every `ReadItem` answered `cost: 1`. With instrumentation included, the 56 reads
  sum to 56 points.
- **Mutations:** a mutation cannot ask for `rateLimit`, so the forge gives no per-mutation cost
  in the body. I read it from the `x-ratelimit-used` header instead. In the quietest window, the
  laptop's setup calls under reset `1795106695`, 30 of the 35 consecutive deltas into a mutation
  were exactly 1, and the rest were 2 or 3. That fits a cost of 1 per mutation plus other users
  of the same account; the sibling spikes ran at the same time on the same login. On that reading
  the 8 mutations cost 8 points, which makes **15 primary points** for the lifecycle. This is a
  measured mode, not a per-call answer.
- **Secondary-limit points:** 7 × 1 + 8 × 5 = **47**, using the per-request costs below.

**Header noise.** During the runs, GraphQL answers came back under two reset values
(`1795138635` with 394 calls, `1795138638` with 104), and `used` went backwards between
consecutive calls. The header counters are therefore not a clean per-call meter on a shared
login.

### One reconcile pass over 30 or more cards

Thirty-one card workflows were open: cards 2–32, with card 1 Done. From 00:47:20 to 00:47:50Z,
one reconcile interval, the call log holds:
- **31 `mirror-read` calls**: one `ReadItem` per card, 31 distinct cards, summed cost 31;
- **0 writes**;
- 31 read-backs, which are instrumentation.

**Per-card route:** one pass over a board of 31 cards with no drift costs **31 GraphQL reads and
0 writes, 31 points**.

**Batched route (measured cost, not run as the reconcile):** **1 GraphQL read, cost 1**, for all
32 items.

## Affordability

**Limits, and where I read them.** I read both docs pages on 2026-11-20, and saw the live
5,000-point limit in the `x-ratelimit-limit` header of every GraphQL answer in the call log.

| Limit | Source |
|---|---|
| GraphQL primary: "5,000 points per hour per user". A query costs at least 1 point. | docs.github.com, "Rate limits and query limits for the GraphQL API" |
| GraphQL secondary: "no more than 2,000 points per minute". A request with no mutation costs 1; one with a mutation costs 5. "No more than 100 concurrent requests". Content creation: "80 … per minute", "500 … per hour". | the same page |
| REST primary: "5,000 requests per hour". Secondary: 900 points per minute, and the same content-creation limits. | docs.github.com, "Rate limits for the REST API" |

**Load.** I use the load the card's "Load" section sets: `concurrency: 3` from
`rigger.config.mjs` at `6be13718` (line 18), and the thirty-card window of
`docs/v0-build-plan.md` §4, M8, test 4. Two premises, both **judgments**:
- Every card in the window is open at once, so 30 card workflows each reconcile. That is the
  worst case for polling.
- At concurrency 3, at most about 9 cards finish an hour, which assumes each card holds a slot
  for at least 20 minutes.

**Per hour, at the measured per-card costs.** The per-lifecycle figures come from card 1's
transitions: 6 transition reads and 7 transition writes.

| Item | Per-card route (what I ran) | Batched route |
|---|---|---|
| Reconcile reads (R = 30 s → 120 passes an hour) | 30 cards × 120 = **3,600** | 1 × 120 = **120** |
| Transitions (≈9 cards an hour × (6 reads + 7 writes)) | 9 × 13 = 117 | 117 |
| Drift reverts (1 write each; judgment: a few an hour) | ≈5 | ≈5 |
| **GraphQL primary points an hour** (1 point per call, as measured) | **≈3,722**, 74% of 5,000 | **≈242**, 4.8% of 5,000 |
| GraphQL secondary, peak per minute (reads ×1, mutations ×5) | 60 reads + ≈2 mutations × 5 ≈ 70 of 2,000 | 2 + ≈10 ≈ 12 of 2,000 |
| Writes an hour, against the content-creation limit of 500 an hour, if field updates count | ≈68 | ≈68 |
| REST calls an hour | 0 of 5,000 | 0 of 5,000 |

## Recommendation

**A one-way board mirror is affordable at this load, provided its reconcile reads the board in
one batched query per pass.** It then costs about 5% of the user's GraphQL hour, with ample room
under every secondary limit.

**Do not use the per-card polling I ran on a token shared with anything else.** At 30 open cards
it takes about three quarters of the hour's 5,000 points. Rigger's own board reads, the gh CLI,
the judges and other sessions spend that same budget, and this run itself shared the login with
two sibling spikes.

The convergence results hold for either reconcile:
- the drift reverted;
- the workflow's state was untouched by board edits;
- answers arrived by signal;
- an I4 kill healed on retry.

For the card that builds this:
- **Do** make the reconcile one paginated board read per pass, compare it to the workflows'
  state, and send the write only for items that drifted.
- **Do** keep `mirrorCard` read-compare-write over full state. Its second run wrote nothing, and
  its retry after a kill repaired a partial write.
- **Do** give the activity a heartbeat timeout. Without one, a killed worker's activity waits out
  its whole `startToCloseTimeout` before it is retried.
- **Do not assume** that a successful mirror run leaves the board equal to the workflow. I2a and
  I3 both ended `match: false`, and only the next reconcile closed the gap. Anything that reads
  the board as the truth will see up to one bound of drift.
- **Do not assume** a webhook can replace the poll for a user-owned board.
- **Do not assume** a mutation's point cost can be read per call. The forge does not return it.

**What would reverse this recommendation.**
- **Batched read cost:** `ScanBoard`'s `rateLimit.cost` grows well past 1 at the window's size.
  I measured cost 1 at 32 items, and the cost formula depends on the connections requested.
- **Faster convergence:** a convergence requirement much tighter than 60 s. At R = 5 s the
  batched route is about 720 points an hour, which is still affordable; the per-card route is
  about 21,600, which is impossible.
- **Shared budget:** the account's other consumers already spend most of the hour.
- **Mutation cost:** evidence that mutations cost more than 1 primary point.
- **Content-creation limits:** that Projects field updates count toward them at a lower limit
  than the documented 500 an hour.
- **Webhooks:** an organization-owned board, where the `projects_v2_item` webhook would remove
  the poll altogether.

## What it would change (proposals; nothing is changed here)

These are proposals for the PM and the architect. This report changes none of them.

- **`R-STATE-1`**, "The board is the truth for a card's workflow state". Under a mirror the
  workflow is the truth, and the board is a mirror that converges within a stated bound.
- **`R-STATE-4`**, which persists only what cannot be rebuilt from the board. Card state would
  then live in Temporal's store, not be rebuilt from the board.
- **`ARCHITECTURE.md`, "The layers"**, the forge-adapter rule that L0 "carries L2's column
  changes back to the board, and no other layer changes a card's column". The column would
  become a projection written by a mirror activity, plus a reconcile that reads the board.
- **`ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Triggers"**, "The board poll". The idle poll that finds Ready cards would
  become the mirror's reconcile read, or would sit beside it.
- **`ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Escalation"**. An owner's answer would arrive as a workflow signal rather
  than through the board and issue.

=== [108] issued 2026-11-20T00:55:55.656Z  answered 2026-11-20T00:58:56.129Z  Bash
description: Write journal entry and run deterministic tests  [timeout=1800000]
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer && cat > docs/journal/2026-11-20-0055-1431-projects-v2-mirror.md <<'EOF'
ABOUTME: Journal entry for card #1431: what the Projects v2 one-way mirror spike over Temporal taught us.

# A Projects v2 board mirrors a Temporal card workflow, at a batched-read price

Spike #1431 ran a Temporal card workflow per card on the Windows machine and mirrored each one to a
scratch Projects v2 board ([report](../spikes/projects-v2-mirror.md)).

**What held.**
- Every API edit away from the workflow's column was reverted by the reconcile within the 60 s bound.
- No board edit changed the workflow's state.
- An escalation answer sent as a CLI signal reached the board in 1.3 s.
- A worker killed between two field writes healed on retry: the retry wrote only the missing field.

**What we learned.**
- **A successful run can leave drift.** A read-compare-write mirror misses an edit that lands after
  its read, in a run that found nothing to write. The run reports success over a drifted board, and
  only the next reconcile closes the gap.
- **The cost is in the polling.** Polling per card at a 30 s interval costs about 3,600 GraphQL
  points an hour at 30 open cards, against 5,000 for the whole account. One batched board read per
  pass costs about 120.
- **The forge does not return a mutation's cost.** `x-ratelimit-used` on a login shared with other
  sessions does not meter cost per call.

**What failed.**
- WSL's sshd was not reachable, so the run moved to native Windows.
- One setup call died with `EHOSTUNREACH` after its issue had already been created. That made setup
  resumable and taught the logger to record failed fetches too.
EOF
date -u +%T; npm test 2>&1 | tail -15

Closes #1431.

This is a docs-only pull request: spike #1431's report, its evidence, a journal entry and the report's `doc-references.json` entry. The throwaway code stayed in the gitignored `spikes/` and is not part of this PR.

**Findings.** The spike ran on the Windows machine (`hostpc`), with a Temporal dev server on ports 7243/8243 and one worker. Each card had one workflow, and the mirror was a scratch Projects v2 board.
- **Drift:** every API drift edit was reverted by the reconcile, in 22.6 s, 35.0 s and 35.0 s, against a 60 s bound.
- **Workflow state:** board edits never changed it, as the event histories show.
- **Escalation answer:** the CLI signal reached the board in 1.3 s.
- **Kill mid-run:** a worker killed between two field writes healed on retry. The retry wrote only the missing field.
- **Interleavings:** in I2 (reconcile run) and I3 the run ended with the board drifted, and the next reconcile closed the gap.
- **Cost of one card:** Ready to Done, with an escalation and a drift, took 15 GraphQL calls (7 reads, 8 writes) and no REST calls.
- **Affordability:** at concurrency 3 and 30 cards the mirror is affordable only with a batched reconcile read, about 242 points an hour. Per-card polling costs about 3,700 of the user's 5,000 points an hour.

**Files.**
- The report: `docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror.md`.
- The evidence, in `docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror/`:
  - `transcript.txt`
  - `calllog.json`
  - `mirror-runs.json`
  - `histories/`
  - `host-logs.txt`
- The journal entry: `docs/journal/2026-11-20-0055-1431-projects-v2-mirror.md`.

The transcript and host logs are `.txt` because `test/path-check.test.mjs` holds every tracked `.md` under `docs/spikes/` to be a checked spike report.

**Deviation from the card.** The scratch repository was not left for the owner. rigger-ae deleted it at the owner's request ([1431#7027544903](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1431#issuecomment-7027544903)). The spike deleted the scratch board, and the forge's not-found read-back is quoted in the report.

=== [165] issued 2026-11-20T16:15:11.156Z  answered 2026-11-20T16:15:11.364Z  Bash
description: Compute rate-limit bounds, concurrency and peaks from call log
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer && python3 - <<'EOF'
import json,collections
from datetime import datetime,timedelta
P=lambda s: datetime.fromisoformat(s.replace('Z','+00:00'))
A=json.load(open('docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror/calllog.json'))['calls']
R=[e for e in A if e['host'].startswith('Host')]
g=[e for e in R if e['api']=='graphql' and e.get('ratelimit') and e['ratelimit'].get('used')]
for b in sorted(set(e['ratelimit']['reset'] for e in g)):
  bb=[e for e in g if e['ratelimit']['reset']==b]
  u=[int(e['ratelimit']['used']) for e in bb]
  q=sum(e['cost'] or 0 for e in bb if e['rw']=='read'); m=sum(1 for e in bb if e['rw']=='write')
  print('bucket',b,len(bb),'reads',sum(1 for e in bb if e['rw']=='read'),'querycost',q,'mutations',m,'used min',min(u),'max',max(u),'first',u[0],'last',u[-1], bb[0]['ts'],bb[-1]['ts'])
# all graphql spike calls in remote window regardless bucket
allg=[e for e in R if e['api']=='graphql']
print('remote graphql calls',len(allg),'reads',sum(e['rw']=='read' for e in allg),'writes',sum(e['rw']=='write' for e in allg), 'querycost', sum(e['cost'] or 0 for e in allg if e['rw']=='read'))
# concurrency: max overlapping in-flight requests
ev=[]
for e in A:
  if 'ms' in e:
    s=P(e['ts']); ev.append((s,1)); ev.append((s+timedelta(milliseconds=e['ms']),-1))
ev.sort(); c=mx=0
for t,d in ev:
  c+=d; mx=max(mx,c)
print('max concurrent requests',mx)
# per-minute rolling peaks (60s windows starting at each call)
ts=[(P(e['ts']),e) for e in R]; ts.sort(key=lambda x:x[0])
best={'all':0,'writes':0,'sec':0,'prodwrites':0}
for i,(t,_) in enumerate(ts):
  w=[e for t2,e in ts[i:] if t2<t+timedelta(seconds=60)]
  allc=len(w); wr=sum(e['rw']=='write' for e in w); sec=sum(5 if e['rw']=='write' else 1 for e in w if e['api']=='graphql')
  pw=sum(e['rw']=='write' and e.get('purpose')=='mirror-write' for e in w)
  for k,v in (('all',allc),('writes',wr),('sec',sec),('prodwrites',pw)):
    if v>best[k]: best[k]=v
print('peak 60s window', best)
EOF

=== [166] issued 2026-11-20T16:15:29.062Z  answered 2026-11-20T16:15:29.268Z  Bash
description: Compute observation bounds for signal and drift timings
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer && python3 - <<'EOF'
import json
from datetime import datetime,timedelta
P=lambda s: datetime.fromisoformat(s.replace('Z','+00:00'))
A=json.load(open('docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror/calllog.json'))['calls']
def end(e): return P(e['ts'])+timedelta(milliseconds=e['ms'])
cases=[('signal','1','2026-11-20T00:42:48.146Z','00:42:49.4'),('I1','1','2026-11-20T00:43:01.675Z','00:43:24.2'),('I2a','2','2026-11-20T00:43:49.893Z','00:44:24.8'),('I3','3','2026-11-20T00:44:41.493Z','00:45:16.5')]
for name,card,t0,match in cases:
  T0=P(t0)
  polls=[e for e in A if e.get('card')==card and e.get('purpose')=='measure' and P(e['ts'])>=T0-timedelta(seconds=1)]
  m=[e for e in polls if e['ts'][11:22]==match[:11] or e['ts'][11:21]==match[:10]]
  mi=polls.index(m[0]); prev=polls[mi-1] if mi>0 else None
  w=[e for e in A if e.get('card')==card and e.get('purpose')=='mirror-write' and T0<=P(e['ts'])<=end(m[0])]
  print(name,'match start',m[0]['ts'],'end',end(m[0]).isoformat(),'ms',(end(m[0])-T0).total_seconds()*1000,
        '| prev poll start',prev['ts'] if prev else None,'prev end',end(prev).isoformat() if prev else None,'lower(ms from prev start)',(P(prev['ts'])-T0).total_seconds()*1000 if prev else None,
        '| writes',[(x['operation'],x['ts'],end(x).isoformat(),(end(x)-T0).total_seconds()*1000) for x in w])
# edit ack: edit-drift entries
for e in A:
  if e.get('purpose')=='edit-drift': print('edit',e['card'],e['ts'],end(e).isoformat(),e['ms'])
EOF

p='forge.js'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("""  const res = await fetch('https://api.github.com' + endpoint, {""","""  const res = await guarded(() => fetch('https://api.github.com' + endpoint, {""")
s=s.replace("""    body: body ? JSON.stringify(body) : undefined,
  });""","""    body: body ? JSON.stringify(body) : undefined,
  }), { ts: t0.toISOString(), api: 'rest', rw: method === 'GET' ? 'read' : 'write', method, endpoint, ...tag });""")
s=s.replace("""  const res = await fetch('https://api.github.com/graphql', {""","""  const res = await guarded(() => fetch('https://api.github.com/graphql', {""")

  });""","""    body: JSON.stringify({ query, variables }),
  }), { ts: t0.toISOString(), api: 'graphql', rw: /^\\s*mutation/.test(query) ? 'write' : 'read', method: 'POST', operation: op, ...tag });""")

// A call that fails below HTTP (DNS, TLS, reset) is still a call that may have landed: log it.
async function guarded(send, entry) {
  try { return await send(); } catch (e) {
    log({ ...entry, status: 'network-error', error: String(e.cause?.code || e.message) });
    throw e;
  }
}""")
open(p,'w').write(s)
p='setup.js'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("""    const issue = await rest('POST', `/repos/${OWNER}/${REPO}/issues`, { title: `Scratch card ${i}` }, tag);""",
"""    // A run cut off after creating issue i but before adding it resumes on that issue.
    const issue = await rest('GET', `/repos/${OWNER}/${REPO}/issues/${i}`, null, tag).catch(() => null)
      || await rest('POST', `/repos/${OWNER}/${REPO}/issues`, { title: `Scratch card ${i}` }, tag);""")
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

=== [180] issued 2026-11-20T16:19:12.257Z  answered 2026-11-20T16:19:12.499Z  Bash
description: Draft verbatim unwrapped-command section
cat > /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-cjwilliams-GitHub-rigger/17bfb854-c7ed-40ce-af2d-5578b0a83820/scratchpad/1431-r1/section.txt <<'SECTION'
---

## Commands run outside the wrapper, verbatim

This section replaces the summary table that stood here at 8fb675a7. It gives each command the spike ran on the laptop without `t.sh`, verbatim, in order. Where a command wrote a file with a heredoc, the file's body follows it.

**What the times are.** A time marked **mtime** is the file's last-modification time on the laptop, read with `TZ=UTC stat -f %Sm`. It is a measurement of the write, unless a later patch, named beside it, moved it. A time marked **between** is a bracket: the wrapped transcript entries immediately before and after the command. Commands of that kind recorded no time of their own, and no exact time can be recovered for them.

**What the bodies are.** Each body below is the file as it stood at the end of the spike. Where a later patch changed a file, the heredoc's original body is the final body with that patch reverted. import json,collections
R=[json.loads(l) for l in open('remote/calllog.jsonl')]+[json.loads(l) for l in open('calllog.jsonl')]

print(collections.Counter(e['ratelimit']['reset'] for e in g))
for reset in set(e['ratelimit']['reset'] for e in g):
  b=[e for e in g if e['ratelimit']['reset']==reset]
  d=collections.defaultdict(list)
  for p,c in zip(b,b[1:]):
    d[c['rw']].append(int(c['ratelimit']['used'])-int(p['ratelimit']['used']))
  print(reset, len(b), {k:collections.Counter(v).most_common(6) for k,v in d.items()})
EOF

    `used` sometimes went backwards between consecutive answers, so the bound above is only as
    good as the first and last readings.

`calllog.json`, over every logged call including instrumentation.

- **Transition burst:** card 1 made its last four transition writes within 47.3 s: Status to
  Review, the escalation clear, Owner and Done, from 00:42:48.425Z to 00:43:35.736Z. At
  concurrency 3, three cards can finish together, so three such spans can align: **12 writes and
  12 reads in one minute**.

  spike did exactly this with 27 cards at 00:45:53Z.

description: Verify measured peak claims and locations
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer && python3 - <<'EOF'
import json
from datetime import datetime,timedelta
P=lambda s: datetime.fromisoformat(s.replace('Z','+00:00'))
A=[e for e in json.load(open('docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror/calllog.json'))['calls'] if not e.get('reconstructed')]
print('remote rest', sum(1 for e in A if e['host'].startswith('Host') and e['api']=='rest'))
ts=sorted(A,key=lambda e:e['ts'])
best={}
for i,e in enumerate(ts):
  t=P(e['ts']); w=[x for x in ts[i:] if P(x['ts'])<t+timedelta(seconds=60)]
  for k,v in (('writes',sum(x['rw']=='write' for x in w)),('sec',sum(5 if x['rw']=='write' else 1 for x in w if x['api']=='graphql'))):
    if v>best.get(k,(0,))[0]: best[k]=(v,e['ts'])
print(best)
ev=[]
for e in A:
  if 'ms' in e: s=P(e['ts']); ev+= [(s,1,e['ts']),(s+timedelta(milliseconds=e['ms']),-1,e['ts'])]
ev.sort(key=lambda x:(x[0],x[1])); c=mx=0; at=None
for t,d,_ in ev:
  c+=d
  if c>mx: mx=c; at=t
print('max inflight',mx,at)
EOF

=== [194] issued 2026-11-20T16:21:42.994Z  answered 2026-11-20T16:21:43.259Z  Bash
description: Recompute measured peaks with tolerant time parsing
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer && python3 - <<'EOF'
import json,re
from datetime import datetime,timedelta
def P(s):
  s=s.replace('Z','')
  if '.' in s: a,b=s.split('.'); s=a+'.'+b.ljust(3,'0')[:6]
  return datetime.fromisoformat(s)
A=[e for e in json.load(open('docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror/calllog.json'))['calls'] if not e.get('reconstructed')]
ts=sorted(A,key=lambda e:e['ts'])
best={}
for i,e in enumerate(ts):
  t=P(e['ts']); w=[x for x in ts[i:] if P(x['ts'])<t+timedelta(seconds=60)]
  for k,v in (('writes',sum(x['rw']=='write' for x in w)),('sec',sum(5 if x['rw']=='write' else 1 for x in w if x['api']=='graphql'))):
    if v>best.get(k,(0,))[0]: best[k]=(v,e['ts'])
print(best)
ev=[]
for e in A:
  if 'ms' in e: s=P(e['ts']); ev+= [(s,1),(s+timedelta(milliseconds=e['ms']),-1)]
ev.sort(); c=mx=0; at=None
for t,d in ev:
  c+=d
  if c>mx: mx=c; at=t
print('max inflight',mx,at)
EOF

**How the two foreground pids are known.** I did not record them at the time. The Temporal
client's default identity is pid@hostname. The histories' `WORKFLOW_EXECUTION_STARTED` events
carry `3180@Host-PC10` (cards 1 and 4) and `11740@Host-PC10` (cards 2, 3 and 5). The worker
identities `34000@Host-PC10` and `30548@Host-PC10` in the same histories match the recorded worker
pids.

**Pids I cannot recover.** Item 43 asks for every process with its pid, and this inventory does
not have them all. Every other process was started by a foreground command that exited before the
next one ran, and nothing recorded its pid:

print('max concurrent requests',mx)

best={'all':0,'writes':0,'sec':0,'prodwrites':0}
for i,(t,_) in enumerate(ts):

for e in A:

import json,sys

from sess import load
C=load()
out=['ABOUTME: Spike 1431 session log: every tool call the spike engineer made, in order, with its exact input and the times it was issued and answered, extracted from the session record.','',
'Source: the Claude Code session record of this spike engineer, subagent a9ff8ffeb5a15463e of session 17bfb854-c7ed-40ce-af2d-5578b0a83820, read on the laptop [laptop host].',
'Each entry gives:',
'- "issued": the timestamp of the assistant message that made the call, in UTC.',
'- "answered": the timestamp of the message that carried its result. For a background command this is when it was launched, not when it ended.',
'- the tool name and its input verbatim. For Bash that is the command string exactly as run on the laptop; a command inside an ssh or w.sh string ran on Host-PC10 (hostpc). A command chained with ; or && inside one call has no time of its own beyond the call\'s.',
'Outputs are not included; transcript.txt holds the wrapped outputs. The record ends at the extraction; the commands after it are listed at the end.','']
for i,c in enumerate(C,1):

  inp=c['input']
  if c['name']=='Bash':
    flags=[k+'='+str(v) for k,v in inp.items() if k not in('command','description')]
    out.append('description: '+inp.get('description','')+('  ['+', '.join(flags)+']' if flags else ''))
    out.append(inp['command'])
  elif c['name']=='Write':
    out.append('file_path: '+inp['file_path']); out.append('content:'); out.append(inp['content'])
  elif c['name']=='Edit':
    out.append('file_path: '+inp['file_path']); out.append('old_string:'); out.append(inp['old_string']); out.append('new_string:'); out.append(inp['new_string'])
  else:
    out.append(json.dumps(inp,indent=1,ensure_ascii=False))
  out.append('')
open('docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror/session-log.txt','w').write('\n'.join(out))
print(len(C))
EOF
wc -c docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror/session-log.txt; grep -nE 'gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}|github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{20,}|Bearer [A-Za-z0-9._-]{20,}|-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY|AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}|xox[baprs]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}|sk-[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}' docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror/session-log.txt | cut -c1-120 | head

    `source` names the entry, and its `note` says which of its fields are not measured.
  - **Completeness:** I checked every `gh`, `git fetch`, `git push` and curl.exe command, and every
    documentation fetch, in session-log.txt through revision 2 against these entries. Each has
    one.
  - **Exceptions:** the revision-2 push, which this file cannot record because the push carries
    it. One `gh issue view 1431 --comments --json comments`, in session-log entry [3], failed on
    its own flags before sending any request; gh printed its usage text.''')
rep('''- The call log's reconstructed entries add four hand-made writes, all on this card or its
  branch:
  - the two stated-intent comments on #1431;
  - the push of `rigger-1431`;
  - pull request #1435.
- This revision's own push is not in the log, because a file cannot record the push that carries
  it. The transcript's revision-1 entries record it.''','''- The call log's reconstructed entries add five hand-made writes, all on this card or its
  branch:

  - pull request #1435.
- The revision-2 push is not in the log, because a file cannot record the push that carries it.
  The transcript's revision-2 section names it.''')
open(p,'w').write(s); print('ok')
